## A1. Event identity, timing and state

- Match: Polona Hercog (SLO) v Varvara Lepchenko (USA); WTA 125 DIRECTV Open Presentado por Compesar / Cundinamarca Open, Round 1, women's singles, Court 2, Sopó, Colombia; outdoor clay, standard best-of-three singles expected. Reference WTA competition page: https://www.wtatennis.com/tournaments/1181/sopo-125/2026
- Primary published match schedule: Monday **9 Nov 2026, 15:00 UTC**, equivalent to **Monday 9 Nov 10:00 local Colombia (UTC−5)** / **Tuesday 10 Nov 02:00 Australia/Melbourne (AEDT)**. Schedule evidence: https://www.wtaschedule.com/wta-matches-today and https://en.tennistemple.com/competitions/sopo-2026/128051/orderofplay and https://virvib.com/sports-live/varvara-lepchenko-vs-polona-hercog-directv-open-presentado-por-compesar-wta-1084-2026-186749 .
- One provider displayed generic “Postponed Suspended” across numerous courts and tournaments, while other live schedules showed “Scheduled”; an independent official actual first-ball timestamp or confirmed revised start was **NOT_VERIFIED**. Tournament status “in progress” is not proof this specific match has started.
- No original native WTA match identifier was independently recovered. Operator settlement terms: `UNKNOWN_DEFINITION`; see A6.

## A2. Participants and availability

- Hercog: age 35, right-handed; recent appearances at Queretaro (Oct 27 and 29), Cali (Nov 1 and 2). Her most recent completed match was a **0–6, 1–6 loss** to Leyre Romero Gormaz on Nov 2. Previous recorded retirements include Kitzbuhel (16 July) and Rabat (18 May), not evidence of a current injury.
- Lepchenko: age 40, left-handed; on Oct 18 and 19 she won Austin qualifying against Cristina Ramos Sierra and Christasha McNeil; on Oct 20 she **withdrew before** the W50 Austin main-draw match against Martina Capurro Taborda. No confirmed medical reason or current fitness clearance. Her last completed match was Oct 19.
- Confirmed exact-match eligibility: both named in the draw; actual court-side presence/warm-up/fitness: `NOT_VERIFIED`; coach/medical statements for this match: `NOT_VERIFIED`. Tennis singles does not have starting XI/bench, but withdrawal and retirement status matter materially.
- Primary player records: https://www.tennislive.net/wta/polona-hercog/ and https://www.tennislive.net/wta/varvara-lepchenko/ . Walkover corroboration: https://www.tennislive.net/wta/match/martina-capurro-taborda-VS-varvara-lepchenko/w50-austin-2026/ and https://umpiry.com/match/2026-10-20/lepchenko-vs-taborda-w50-austin-r32 .

## A4. Joint match process, forecast reference and calculations

- Full repository reference tier: `NOT_COMPUTED`; reference stage `NOT_RUN`; declared league width floor `NOT_VERIFIED`; expected games by player / expected total / expected sets / P(deciding set): `NOT_COMPUTED`; numerical fit/simulation: `NOT_RUN`.
- **Qualitative shared branches:** (i) Hercog solid clay return and two-set win; (ii) Lepchenko left-handed serve/return rhythm and two-set upset; (iii) close sets producing 7–5/7–6; (iv) three-set win by either; (v) withdrawal or retirement with operator-specific action. No unsupported branch weights have been assigned.
- Illustrative *scenarios, not predictions*: Hercog **6–4, 6–4** gives aggregate +4 games and 20 total: Hercog ML, Hercog −1.5, Under 21.5. Hercog **6–4, 3–6, 6–4** gives aggregate +1 and 29 total: Hercog ML but **Lepchenko +1.5 games** and **Over**. Lepchenko **6–3, 6–4** yields Lepchenko ML, Lepchenko +1.5 and Under. These demonstrate that ML and game handicap are not identical, and a third set changes the total strongly.
- The raw full-season clay record favours Hercog, while the 2 Nov surface Elo benchmark is almost flat. Both sides therefore retain plausible two-set and deciding-set win paths; low confidence / broad uncertainty is compulsory.

## A5. Exact contracts and directional comparison

- Winner: Hercog ML versus Lepchenko ML; neither has a certified p. Directional preference Hercog **very slight only**. No draw in a completed best-of-three singles match.
- **Games handicap, not set handicap:** Hercog −1.5 games succeeds if (total games Hercog − total games Lepchenko) > 1.5 at the applicable settlement endpoint; Lepchenko +1.5 succeeds when that difference is < 1.5. Half-game line has no push after an ordinary completed match. The −1.5 game cover must never be assumed just because Hercog wins a three-set match.
- Combined games O/U **21.5**: Over when sum of ordinary games >= 22; Under when <= 21; tiebreak **points** are excluded (a 7–6 set contains 13 games). No push on a half-game total after normal match completion.
- Low-confidence qualitative ranking: **Hercog ML; Hercog −1.5 games; Under 21.5 games**. The exact ordering of the last two is unproven; **NO_STRONG_PICK / ABSTAIN** governs. No unverified claim of >65% selection confidence or expected betting value.

## A6. Court, weather, format and settlement

- Outdoor clay, 32-player singles main draw; 125-level tournament; first round. A venue-local forecast page suggested temperatures near 19°C and modest wind around local 10:00, but neither court-level conditions nor rain interruption was independently corroborated. Weather adjustment: **NOT_ESTIMATED**. Source: https://www.meteocity.com/colombie/sopo-v3667807 .
- Expected standard advantage-scoring best of three sets with regular tiebreak at 6–6; exact deciding-set rules and operator-specific definitions were **NOT_VERIFIED**. Do not substitute a 10-point match tiebreak for a normal third set without proof.
- **Operator retirement/walkover terms UNKNOWN_DEFINITION:** Moneyline, game handicap and total may void or settle differently if either player withdraws/retires. A sporting advancement result does not establish the wager outcome. No wager is recommended on an unstated operator convention.

## A7. Bust paths / sensitivity

1. Hercog's recent **0–6, 1–6** loss signals possible deterioration or matchup variance, which could overwhelm her historical clay edge.
2. Lepchenko's **20 October withdrawal** is a genuine participation/fitness risk, but cause and persistence are unknown. Do not call her injured.
3. Opponent strength and qualifying-level differences can reverse raw W–L comparisons. The independent clay Elo gap is only 4.1 points.
4. A competitive tiebreak or any ordinary deciding-set match may send the total Over 21.5 despite a straight-sets/Under lean.
5. A three-set Hercog win with just a +1 game margin wins the ML but loses −1.5 games.
6. Delay, rain, late withdrawal or mid-match retirement could alter match exposure and all contract settlements.

**Model reference stage:** `NOT_RUN` — repo source exists on GitHub in read-only mode, but the operator's local working tree, current data snapshots, and required `python -m ml reference tier`, `reference run`, `card lint`, `pregame run` receipts are not present. - Bendigo current offense 313/4 = 78.25; defense 359/4 = 89.75.

This diagnostic lacks league normalization, opponent adjustment, home strength, current availability-mixture coefficients, pace, possessions, covariance, overtime, and a reference width. It must NOT be promoted to a forecast or read as the p_card anchor.
- Alternative qualitative routes: (i) depleted Bendigo halfcourt scoring and slow pace => Under and a potentially large Perth margin; (ii) Perth transition success versus short Bendigo bench => higher Perth points, Perth cover/Over; (iii) Bendigo's Jade Melbourne offense sustaining competitive periods => +10.5 Bendigo and potentially Over; (iv) Perth early fouls/bench rotations => narrower margin regardless of total.
- No declared numeric scenario weights, SDs, expected scoring intervals, reference p, baseline p, or W/L probability masses could be evidenced. They remain `NOT_COMPUTED` / `NOT_ESTIMATED`, not 0.5. The supplied lines are contract descriptions, not odds or probabilities.

## A6 — Winner, spread, total and interdependence

- Winner direction: Perth (home, roster availability, rest, Bendigo 0–4), uncalibrated and not a supported probability.
- Perth −10.5: Requires winning by **11 or more**, including OT. Perth's 2026–27 wins were by 3 and 8, but over different opponents and split venues. Bendigo lost its four games by 2, 23, 7 and 14, all outside Perth. The large handicap does not follow merely from a likely Perth win.
- Bendigo +10.5: Covers on a Bendigo victory or a Perth win by 1–10. If Bendigo's shortened rotation sustains offense and defensive rebounding, this remains plausible. A decisive Perth run and thin Bendigo bench can break the cushion.
- Over 167.5: Current Perth games 169, 172, 185 (3/3 Over); Bendigo 186, 159, 147, 180 (2/4 Over). The naive unadjusted matchup arithmetic is 171.7. Both the historical head-to-head 159 average and Bendigo's low-scoring losses challenge that signal.
- Under 167.5: Supported by all four recent prior-season H2Hs and two Bendigo current games, but the large post-season changes to both teams preclude treating those results as a current pace model.
- Dependence: Perth −10.5 and the Under can both win through Bendigo offensive suppression; Perth −10.5 and Over can both win through Perth efficiency; Bendigo +10.5 and Over can coexist in a competitive high-scoring game. Multiple directions are NOT independent legs and are NOT recommended as a parlay.

## A5. Descriptive calculation and contract dependence

- **Cross-match season scoring:** Mobis estimated PF = (Mobis PF 83.5 + Sono PA 90.8)/2 = 87.15. Sono estimated PF = (Sono PF 89.6 + Mobis PA 88.7)/2 = 89.15. **Total 176.30**, Sono margin +2.00. This double-counts shared league context and does not adjust today's rosters.
- **Pace / efficiency alternative:** Assumed blended pace (75.1+72.9)/2 = 74 possessions. Mobis points ~74×(108.4+121.1)/(2×100) = 84.915. Sono ~74×(118.8+115.2)/(2×100) = 86.58. **Total ~171.50**, Sono margin ~+1.67. Only a rough arithmetic check, not a calibrated possession model; published ratings can use different pace denominators.
- **L10 totals:** Mobis 83.0+87.9=170.9, Sono 91.8+92.4=184.2. Equal-weight descriptive midpoint **177.55**. The L5 combined means are **168.2** and **195.0**, so the midpoint **181.6** overweights the same short-term window and a previous roster regime.
- **Count comparison to line:** Mobis L5: 2 Over / 3 Under 175.5. Sono L5: 4 Over / 1 Under 175.5. H2H 177, barely Over. These are dependent descriptive counts, not a model-based probability.
- **Representative joint states (unweighted, not forecasts):** Sono 88–86 Mobis (174 total, Mobis +3 and Under), Sono 94–85 Mobis (179, Sono −3 and Over), Mobis 89–85 Sono (174, Mobis +3 and Under), Sono 91–88 Mobis (179; spread PUSH, total Over). No weights because an empirical and independently qualified point/margin distribution is unavailable.
- **Overtime:** user-provided lines include OT; explicit tie mass, OT possessions and correlations not estimated, which is a material unresolved numerical issue.
- **No numeric P/W/P/L:** a proper reference computation, verified minute-level roster weights, common variance parameters and actual outcome state were not obtained. Any numeric winner/spread/total probabilities would be invented.

- Mobis coach Yang Dong-geun; Sono coach Son Chang-hwan, as credited in 10 November BasketKorea preview. - Weather is irrelevant to an indoor match; travel/rest information above lacks a measured adjustment. No referee assignment verified, and no precise last-minute starter list verified.

## A5. H2H, venue, schedule and market dependence

- October 10 first meeting of 2026–27: **Toronto 6–3 Colorado** in Colorado; total 9 goals, Colorado ML lost, Toronto +1.5 covered, Over 6.5 won. This is one game under another goalie and roster regime, not a deterministic rule.
- Last known Colorado result DET shutout Nov 7; Toronto lost NJD 0–4 Nov 8 and entered on three straight defeats. Colorado starts a three-game Canadian road trip. Neither team on a back-to-back; Toronto has a little less rest but remains at home. Colorado home/away split before the game: road 3–5–0 and home 7–1–2 per available season stats, a caution against road favourite certainty.
- Colorado ML and Toronto +1.5 can **both win** on a one-goal Colorado victory. A Toronto outright win defeats Colorado ML but covers Toronto +1.5; a Colorado multi-goal win defeats the cushion. One-goal losses, 3v3 overtime, shootout, empty-net scores and trailing-goalie pull need joint-model treatment.
- Total Over 6.5 can be reached by 4–3, 5–2, 5–3 or 6–2; Under by 3–2, 4–2, 3–3 before OT resolution, etc. A high-event Toronto comeback might simultaneously favour Toronto cushion and Over; Colorado runaway can favour COL ML and Over or even Under if Toronto is suppressed. Empty net can add a late goal or wider margin. No independent-proposition assumption.

- L10 cross-match arithmetic: `(COL 3.6 + TOR conceded 3.2)/2 = 3.40`; `(TOR 3.5 + COL conceded 3.2)/2 = 3.35`; combined **6.75**.
- L5 cross-match arithmetic: `(COL 3.6 + TOR conceded 3.6)/2 = 3.60`; `(TOR 2.4 + COL conceded 2.8)/2 = 2.60`; combined **6.20**. Opposing regime direction warns against overstating the Over at 6.5.
- Reported goalies and unconfirmed forward rotations materially change tails. ## A5 — Offence, defence, xG, special teams, baseline arithmetic and total logic

Season actual rates (team A at Abbotsford, team B Colorado):
- ABB goals scored=42/12=**3.5000**; conceded=32/12=**2.6667**. COL scored=40/14=**2.8571**; conceded=35/14=**2.5000**.
- **Plain opponent cross-matched actual-goal diagnostic:** projected ABB = (3.5000 + 2.5000)/2=**3.0000**; COL=(2.8571+2.6667)/2=**2.7619**; combined=**5.7619**, unadjusted. This is not an approved competition reference, and ignores opponent mix, goalies and outliers.
- **Expected-goals-strength diagnostic:** ABB xGF/60=2.85, COL xGA/60=2.90 -> mean ABB 2.875; COL xGF/60=2.65, ABB xGA/60=2.60 -> mean COL 2.625; combined **5.500**. This is an unadjusted average of reported xG inputs, **not** a score-grid, qualified model, or a forecast probability. Source: https://www.ahldashboard.com/teams/1168?tab=stats ; https://www.ahldashboard.com/teams/1176?tab=stats .
- Expected-goals-share: ABB **52.27%**, COL **47.74%**. Scoring-chance share ABB **53.14%**, COL **45.45%** (different share definitions/sources, so not constrained to sum to exactly 100%).
- Special teams: ABB PP **7/45=15.56%**, PK **33/39=84.62%**; COL PP **9/64=14.06%**, PK **53/61=86.89%**. Both PP rates modest; COL PK more effective, limiting ABB special-team upside. Special-team opportunity volume for this exact game NOT_VERIFIED; do not apply a fictional fixed goal shift.
- League-wide current AHL summary gives **5.83 goals/game in regulation**; the specific league/period cohort, full-game shootout-awarded goals and data lineage must be reconciled before a model prior. https://pitchlabstats.com/hockey-league/ahl .
- The 5.5 total sits between several close diagnostics (5.50 xG, 5.76 cross-match, 5.83 league). The near-horizon combined totals (COL L5 4.8, ABB L5 5.0) lean below the line but are a small, overlapping sample. ***Neither a robust Over nor a robust Under***. OT/SO and empty-net tails alter total 5.5 behaviour.
- **Numerical reference stage:** `python -m ml reference tier --competition AHL` and `reference run` NOT_EXECUTED; league-only baseline parameters NOT_FROZEN; no `ANALYST_PRIMARY_V1` p_card, code score grid, adjustment ledger, simulation seed/N, Monte Carlo error, game-level posterior or adjusted expected margin calculated. Two prior **2026–27 head-to-head** matches (Colorado home): **9 October COL 4–3 ABB (shootout)**, **10 October ABB 4–3 COL (regulation)**. Both ended with seven in listed final score. Winner has mutually exclusive home and away control / regulation-draw-to-OT branches; either team can win quickly or via OT. Total plausible paths include 3–2 ABB (ABB ML, Under 5.5), 4–2 ABB (ABB ML, Over 5.5), 3–2 COL (COL ML, Under 5.5), and 4–3 either team (Over 5.5). The moneyline and total are **not statistically independent**: trailing-team goalie pulls, empty-net goals, score state and OT/SO create correlated tails. Goals from shootout settlement depend on actual operator convention.

**Per-team overlapping current-season windows:**
- Creighton L5/L10/L15/L20 each W2–L0, n=2; points for `(76+83)/2=79.5`, against `(61+55)/2=58.0`, total `(137+138)/2=137.5`. Over 159.5: 0/2; Under: 2/2.
- Kansas State L5/L10/L15/L20 each W1–L1, n=2; points for `(66+95)/2=80.5`, against `(68+78)/2=73.0`, total `(134+173)/2=153.5`. Over 159.5: 1/2; Under: 1/2.
- Combined *four nonindependent team-games* Under 159.5 count 3/4 and Over count 1/4, descriptive only; not a binomial probability estimate and not opponent adjusted.

## A4. Distribution, reference and numerical diagnostics

**Reference stage:** `NOT_EXECUTED`; no local authorised working tree with executable ML reference receipt, no `python -m ml reference tier`, no competition baseline receipt, no official card; hence no published `ANALYST_PRIMARY_V1 p_card`, no Poisson/normal/Monte Carlo run, no uncertainty interval masquerading as statistical confidence. KBL/WNBL/NBL/other basketball competition models are not transported to NCAAB. The repository `research/model_registry/ML_PROMOTIONS.csv` has only its header; no competition-specific fitted model is promoted.

**Raw scoring versus opposing allowance calculation (transparent, not a calibrated forecast):**
- Creighton descriptive scoring centre: `(79.5 scored + 73.0 Kansas State conceded)/2 = 76.25`.
- Kansas State descriptive scoring centre: `(80.5 scored + 58.0 Creighton conceded)/2 = 69.25`.
- Descriptive sum: **145.50**, margin Creighton **+7.00**. The distance from 159.5 is **14.0 points**.
- **Major limitation:** Creighton defence is based on Kansas City and Le Moyne only; Kansas State scored 95 against CSUN with a high-variance 17/41 3PT performance, while Creighton is playing on the road for the first time. Neither an established opponent-adjusted efficiency baseline nor home uplift has been estimated. This centre is a *diagnostic*, not a rankable forecast.
- An Under and Creighton cover can coexist (e.g. 79–73, total 152, margin 6); a Kansas State hot shooting/fast-paced branch can produce Over and Kansas State +3.5. They are dependent alternatives. Overtime is an explicit unresolved upside total tail, not silently excluded.
- **Baseline_P, p_card, score distribution widths, overtime frequency, covariance, possession count:** `NOT_COMPUTED / NOT_VERIFIED` for this exact league/regime. No simulation performed.

## A5. Contract-specific failure branches and sensitivity

1. **Creighton −3.5:** wins if Creighton wins by 4+ after any OT. Drivers: two early clean home wins, Enis production, more steals/defensive pressure. Failure: K-State's home perimeter conversion, Creighton first away appearance and new rotation.
2. **Kansas State +3.5:** wins on K-State win or defeat by 1–3; loses on Creighton margin 4+. Drivers: home court and Rechsteiner shooting; failure: K-State's shot-selection volatility and an early deficit from turnovers.
3. **Over 159.5:** 160+ combined. Needs fast conversion, elevated second-half pace/fouls, high 3PT or overtime. This is a plausible but not established state.
4. **Under 159.5:** ≤159 points. Supported by 137, 138 and 134 totals in three pregame matches, Creighton half-court defending and a raw 145.5 diagnostic. Main failure: K-State's 17-of-41 3-point form against CSUN, late fouling, overtime, or Creighton's early defence regressing against better competition.
5. **Winner:** slight Creighton lean; do not confuse projected winner with a successful −3.5 point cover.
6. Any correlation between the preferred Under and preferred Creighton handicap is unmeasured; don't treat as independent legs.

Hercog **6–4, 3–6, 6–4** gives aggregate +1 and 29 total: Hercog ML but **Lepchenko +1.5 games** and **Over**. Lepchenko **6–3, 6–4** yields Lepchenko ML, Lepchenko +1.5 and Under. These demonstrate that ML and game handicap are not identical, and a third set changes the total strongly.
- The raw full-season clay record favours Hercog, while the 2 Nov surface Elo benchmark is almost flat. Both sides therefore retain plausible two-set and deciding-set win paths; low confidence / broad uncertainty is compulsory.

## A5. Exact contracts and directional comparison

- Winner: Hercog ML versus Lepchenko ML; neither has a certified p. Directional preference Hercog **very slight only**. No draw in a completed best-of-three singles match.
- **Games handicap, not set handicap:** Hercog −1.5 games succeeds if (total games Hercog − total games Lepchenko) > 1.5 at the applicable settlement endpoint; Lepchenko +1.5 succeeds when that difference is < 1.5. Half-game line has no push after an ordinary completed match. The −1.5 game cover must never be assumed just because Hercog wins a three-set match.
- Combined games O/U **21.5**: Over when sum of ordinary games >= 22; Under when <= 21; tiebreak **points** are excluded (a 7–6 set contains 13 games). No push on a half-game total after normal match completion.
- Low-confidence qualitative ranking: **Hercog ML; Hercog −1.5 games; Under 21.5 games**. The exact ordering of the last two is unproven; **NO_STRONG_PICK / ABSTAIN** governs. No unverified claim of >65% selection confidence or expected betting value.

## A7. Bust paths / sensitivity

1. Hercog's recent **0–6, 1–6** loss signals possible deterioration or matchup variance, which could overwhelm her historical clay edge.
2. Lepchenko's **20 October withdrawal** is a genuine participation/fitness risk, but cause and persistence are unknown. Do not call her injured.
3. Opponent strength and qualifying-level differences can reverse raw W–L comparisons. The independent clay Elo gap is only 4.1 points.
4. A competitive tiebreak or any ordinary deciding-set match may send the total Over 21.5 despite a straight-sets/Under lean.
5. A three-set Hercog win with just a +1 game margin wins the ML but loses −1.5 games.
6. Delay, rain, late withdrawal or mid-match retirement could alter match exposure and all contract settlements.

## A5 — Joint-score numerical reference attempt / scenario

**Model reference stage:** `NOT_RUN` — repo source exists on GitHub in read-only mode, but the operator's local working tree, current data snapshots, and required `python -m ml reference tier`, `reference run`, `card lint`, `pregame run` receipts are not present. No Monte Carlo, simulation, fitting, calibrated probability or issued p_card may be claimed.

**Descriptive, non-primary arithmetic only:**
- Perth current offense 262/3 = 87.33; defense 264/3 = 88.00.
- Bendigo current offense 313/4 = 78.25; defense 359/4 = 89.75.
- Naive equal-weight offense/opponent-defense crossmatch: Perth = (87.33 + 89.75)/2 = **88.54**; Bendigo = (78.25 + 88.00)/2 = **83.13**; total = **171.67**; margin = **Perth +5.42**. This diagnostic lacks league normalization, opponent adjustment, home strength, current availability-mixture coefficients, pace, possessions, covariance, overtime, and a reference width. It must NOT be promoted to a forecast or read as the p_card anchor.
- Alternative qualitative routes: (i) depleted Bendigo halfcourt scoring and slow pace => Under and a potentially large Perth margin; (ii) Perth transition success versus short Bendigo bench => higher Perth points, Perth cover/Over; (iii) Bendigo's Jade Melbourne offense sustaining competitive periods => +10.5 Bendigo and potentially Over; (iv) Perth early fouls/bench rotations => narrower margin regardless of total.
- No declared numeric scenario weights, SDs, expected scoring intervals, reference p, baseline p, or W/L probability masses could be evidenced. They remain `NOT_COMPUTED` / `NOT_ESTIMATED`, not 0.5. The supplied lines are contract descriptions, not odds or probabilities.

## A6 — Winner, spread, total and interdependence

- Winner direction: Perth (home, roster availability, rest, Bendigo 0–4), uncalibrated and not a supported probability.
- Perth −10.5: Requires winning by **11 or more**, including OT. Perth's 2026–27 wins were by 3 and 8, but over different opponents and split venues. Bendigo lost its four games by 2, 23, 7 and 14, all outside Perth. The large handicap does not follow merely from a likely Perth win.
- Bendigo +10.5: Covers on a Bendigo victory or a Perth win by 1–10. If Bendigo's shortened rotation sustains offense and defensive rebounding, this remains plausible. A decisive Perth run and thin Bendigo bench can break the cushion.
- Over 167.5: Current Perth games 169, 172, 185 (3/3 Over); Bendigo 186, 159, 147, 180 (2/4 Over). The naive unadjusted matchup arithmetic is 171.7. Both the historical head-to-head 159 average and Bendigo's low-scoring losses challenge that signal.
- Under 167.5: Supported by all four recent prior-season H2Hs and two Bendigo current games, but the large post-season changes to both teams preclude treating those results as a current pace model.
- Dependence: Perth −10.5 and the Under can both win through Bendigo offensive suppression; Perth −10.5 and Over can both win through Perth efficiency; Bendigo +10.5 and Over can coexist in a competitive high-scoring game. Multiple directions are NOT independent legs and are NOT recommended as a parlay.

## A7 — Coaching, format, season/league conditions, market exclusion

- Perth coach Ryan Petrik and his focus on the team's defence/foul issues confirmed in Perth's 4 Nov report. No verified game-day tactical scheme, minute allocation or confirmed pace forecast.
- 40-minute FIBA-style regulation, plus any OT under BK-OT-1 (framework rule); no confirmed referees or game-specific opening lineups.
- Supplied **−10.5 / +10.5 and 167.5** used only as exact contract thresholds. No external odds, prices, lines, betting-model or tips content has been used to set directions.
- Form windows, combined team pace, ORtg/DRtg and player minutes not separately confirmed with denominators. The seven recent games are not enough to estimate those reliably as a certification-grade numeric reference.

# READ_ONLY_DRAFT / NOT_ISSUED — Ulsan Hyundai Mobis Phoebus vs Goyang Sono Skygunners

**Authority and scope.** Sports-Research `main` at commit `f79559b45183ca63a2ce5fc2611321975bae744f`, read through the connected GitHub integration exclusively using read actions. Current published controls: MDS-2026.11.10-v24.12; CR-2026.11.10-R2; P-2026.11.10-v24.10; SCV-2026.10.22-v12; SEL-2026.10.22-v4. Referenced `CORE.md`, `CURRENT_STATE.md`, `research/prompts/1_GAME_CARD.md`, `research/prompts/1_sports/BASKETBALL.md`, `RULES_BASKETBALL.md`, and the complete published `research/active_minis/20261109R3NEXT.md`. This is an off-repository research draft, not an issued game card, pregame freeze or certified forecast. GitHub writes/commits/pushes = **NONE**. Calculations described as descriptive heuristics are not the repository reference engine. Formal source-audit, league-reference, linter and model were **NOT_RUN**.

## A1. Event identity / timing

- **Fixture:** Ulsan Hyundai Mobis Phoebus (home) vs Goyang Sono Skygunners (away), Korean Basketball League, 2026–27 regular season. Match identifier reported by StatGG: `S49G01N63`.
- **Scheduled:** Tuesday, 10 November 2026, 19:00 KST (10:00 UTC; 21:00 AEDT). Venue: Dongcheon Gymnasium, Ulsan, South Korea. Official KBL club site and the contemporary Jumpball 10 November preview support the fixture.
- **Actual first play:** NOT_VERIFIED. A local clock read 21:00:48 AEDT after the scheduled start during research. Consequently, **not claimed as a pre-start-issued forecast**. There was no in-game scoring deliberately incorporated. Any later review must use the independently verified actual first-play timestamp; do not backdate issue or inputs.
- **State on the pre-match sources:** listed as scheduled; this is not a claim that the game remained unstarted at final document time. No current-game score or result is used.

All supplied full-game contracts include overtime per the owner's BK-OT-1 convention. KBL regulation: four 10-minute quarters, followed by 5-minute overtime periods when necessary. No separate operator-specific void/settlement agreement was supplied.

**Preferred supplied contract if forced to choose:** Mobis +3.0 (low conviction). **Formal framework decision:** `NO_STRONG_PICK / ABSTAIN`; no supported reference or normalized distribution / probabilities produced. A Goyang 88–86 Mobis outcome (174 combined) is only a structural illustration: Goyang ML, Mobis +3.0 and Under 175.5 can all win together; it is NOT a predicted score.

## A3. Roster, availability, minutes, confirmed players

- **Mobis:** Gaige Prim and Darius Bazley were reported out with injuries assessed around six weeks each. Replacement import Omari Spellman played two games, averaging 28.0 points, 8.0 rebounds and approximately 39 minutes, including 33 points against Anyang on 8 November. Lee Woo-suk's season average is 15.1 points. The 10 November specialist preview says the second import replacement had not yet been secured. Exact starting five and active bench for this event: **NOT_VERIFIED**.
- **Sono:** Scotty James was confirmed sidelined with a lumbar transverse process fracture (about six weeks). Johnny O'Bryant resumed team training before 7 November and played that game; the specialist 10 November preview expects him to be Sono's only active foreign player. This supersedes an older or stale aggregator marking him 'questionable'. Lee Jung-hyun scored double digits in nine consecutive games, averaging about 21 over that stretch. Choi Seung-wook missed 7 November because of a calf injury; exact 10 November status: **NOT_VERIFIED**. Exact starting five and active bench: **NOT_VERIFIED**.
- **Availability effect:** both teams have lost import depth; Mobis relies heavily on Spellman, while Sono's 3-point attack and O'Bryant/Lee Jung-hyun remain available as of specialist reporting. This fact alone has no empirically validated KBL point adjustment in this session; no numerical injury effect is claimed.

## A5. Descriptive calculation and contract dependence

- **Cross-match season scoring:** Mobis estimated PF = (Mobis PF 83.5 + Sono PA 90.8)/2 = 87.15. Sono estimated PF = (Sono PF 89.6 + Mobis PA 88.7)/2 = 89.15. **Total 176.30**, Sono margin +2.00. This double-counts shared league context and does not adjust today's rosters.
- **Pace / efficiency alternative:** Assumed blended pace (75.1+72.9)/2 = 74 possessions. Mobis points ~74×(108.4+121.1)/(2×100) = 84.915. Sono ~74×(118.8+115.2)/(2×100) = 86.58. **Total ~171.50**, Sono margin ~+1.67. Only a rough arithmetic check, not a calibrated possession model; published ratings can use different pace denominators.
- **L10 totals:** Mobis 83.0+87.9=170.9, Sono 91.8+92.4=184.2. Equal-weight descriptive midpoint **177.55**. The L5 combined means are **168.2** and **195.0**, so the midpoint **181.6** overweights the same short-term window and a previous roster regime.
- **Count comparison to line:** Mobis L5: 2 Over / 3 Under 175.5. Sono L5: 4 Over / 1 Under 175.5. H2H 177, barely Over. These are dependent descriptive counts, not a model-based probability.
- **Representative joint states (unweighted, not forecasts):** Sono 88–86 Mobis (174 total, Mobis +3 and Under), Sono 94–85 Mobis (179, Sono −3 and Over), Mobis 89–85 Sono (174, Mobis +3 and Under), Sono 91–88 Mobis (179; spread PUSH, total Over). No weights because an empirical and independently qualified point/margin distribution is unavailable.
- **Overtime:** user-provided lines include OT; explicit tie mass, OT possessions and correlations not estimated, which is a material unresolved numerical issue.
- **No numeric P/W/P/L:** a proper reference computation, verified minute-level roster weights, common variance parameters and actual outcome state were not obtained. Any numeric winner/spread/total probabilities would be invented.

## A6. Coaching, tactics, playing conditions

- Mobis coach Yang Dong-geun; Sono coach Son Chang-hwan, as credited in 10 November BasketKorea preview. Mobis' limited import depth and 40-minute reliance on Spellman creates high minutes/fatigue sensitivity. Sono leads the KBL in 3-point volume according to Jumpball, averaging 14.6 made threes across its first 13 games, 36.5%; Mobis has allowed a league-low 8.6 made threes/game but gave up 16 to Sono on 16 October. This is a material matchup/regime conflict and widens the total/side outlook.
- Four 10-minute quarters; full-game overtime included under BK-OT-1. Late fouls, import substitutions, poor defensive rebounding, free throws and OT can lift the total. A slower half-court/one-import alignment can lower the total.
- Weather is irrelevant to an indoor match; travel/rest information above lacks a measured adjustment. No referee assignment verified, and no precise last-minute starter list verified.

**Toronto projected lines:** Cowan–Matthews–Roslovic; Tverberg–Tavares–Nylander; Joshua–Paul–McKenna; Duhaime–Blueger–MacEwen. Defence Ekman-Larsson–Tanev; McCabe–Raddysh; Mermis–Stecher. Treat playing status as `PROJECTED_RETURN / NOT_FINAL_CONFIRMED`, not absent and not confirmed dressed. Chris Tanev returning from load management. Official projected lineup is NOT a confirmed starting five in basketball terms; the relevant NHL equivalent is final dressed roster plus goalie confirmed.

## A5. H2H, venue, schedule and market dependence

- October 10 first meeting of 2026–27: **Toronto 6–3 Colorado** in Colorado; total 9 goals, Colorado ML lost, Toronto +1.5 covered, Over 6.5 won. This is one game under another goalie and roster regime, not a deterministic rule.
- Last known Colorado result DET shutout Nov 7; Toronto lost NJD 0–4 Nov 8 and entered on three straight defeats. Colorado starts a three-game Canadian road trip. Neither team on a back-to-back; Toronto has a little less rest but remains at home. Colorado home/away split before the game: road 3–5–0 and home 7–1–2 per available season stats, a caution against road favourite certainty.
- Colorado ML and Toronto +1.5 can **both win** on a one-goal Colorado victory. A Toronto outright win defeats Colorado ML but covers Toronto +1.5; a Colorado multi-goal win defeats the cushion. One-goal losses, 3v3 overtime, shootout, empty-net scores and trailing-goalie pull need joint-model treatment.
- Total Over 6.5 can be reached by 4–3, 5–2, 5–3 or 6–2; Under by 3–2, 4–2, 3–3 before OT resolution, etc. A high-event Toronto comeback might simultaneously favour Toronto cushion and Over; Colorado runaway can favour COL ML and Over or even Under if Toronto is suppressed. Empty net can add a late goal or wider margin. No independent-proposition assumption.

## A6. Descriptive scoring budgets (not forecast probabilities)

- Season cross-match arithmetic: COL point-rate midpoint `(COL GF~4.0 + TOR GA~3.3) / 2 ≈ 3.65`; TOR point-rate midpoint `(TOR GF~3.4 + COL GA~3.1) / 2 ≈ 3.25`; combined ≈**6.90**. This is a raw heuristic, **not** an NHL reference model and not a fitted or opponent-adjusted projection.
- L10 cross-match arithmetic: `(COL 3.6 + TOR conceded 3.2)/2 = 3.40`; `(TOR 3.5 + COL conceded 3.2)/2 = 3.35`; combined **6.75**.
- L5 cross-match arithmetic: `(COL 3.6 + TOR conceded 3.6)/2 = 3.60`; `(TOR 2.4 + COL conceded 2.8)/2 = 2.60`; combined **6.20**. Opposing regime direction warns against overstating the Over at 6.5.
- Reported goalies and unconfirmed forward rotations materially change tails. No true conditional hockey goal grid, correlation/covariance, Poisson rate calibrated to correct league reference, OT/SO branch, 6-on-5 empty-net branch, Monte Carlo, Poisson tail or 1-goal-margin mass was run; none is claimed.

Total plausible paths include 3–2 ABB (ABB ML, Under 5.5), 4–2 ABB (ABB ML, Over 5.5), 3–2 COL (COL ML, Under 5.5), and 4–3 either team (Over 5.5). The moneyline and total are **not statistically independent**: trailing-team goalie pulls, empty-net goals, score state and OT/SO create correlated tails. Goals from shootout settlement depend on actual operator convention.

- **Venue:** Bramlage Coliseum, Manhattan, Kansas, US. Kansas State official game centre: https://www.kstatesports.com/game-center/19321 . Kansas State official game notes: https://www.kstatesports.com/news/2026/11/9/mens-basketball-k-state-hosts-creighton-in-nationally-ranked-matchup-on-fs1 .

## A4. Distribution, reference and numerical diagnostics

**Reference stage:** `NOT_EXECUTED`; no local authorised working tree with executable ML reference receipt, no `python -m ml reference tier`, no competition baseline receipt, no official card; hence no published `ANALYST_PRIMARY_V1 p_card`, no Poisson/normal/Monte Carlo run, no uncertainty interval masquerading as statistical confidence. The repository `research/model_registry/ML_PROMOTIONS.csv` has only its header; no competition-specific fitted model is promoted.

**Raw scoring versus opposing allowance calculation (transparent, not a calibrated forecast):**
- Creighton descriptive scoring centre: `(79.5 scored + 73.0 Kansas State conceded)/2 = 76.25`.
- Kansas State descriptive scoring centre: `(80.5 scored + 58.0 Creighton conceded)/2 = 69.25`.
- Descriptive sum: **145.50**, margin Creighton **+7.00**. The distance from 159.5 is **14.0 points**.
- **Major limitation:** Creighton defence is based on Kansas City and Le Moyne only; Kansas State scored 95 against CSUN with a high-variance 17/41 3PT performance, while Creighton is playing on the road for the first time. Neither an established opponent-adjusted efficiency baseline nor home uplift has been estimated. This centre is a *diagnostic*, not a rankable forecast.
- An Under and Creighton cover can coexist (e.g. 79–73, total 152, margin 6); a Kansas State hot shooting/fast-paced branch can produce Over and Kansas State +3.5. They are dependent alternatives. Overtime is an explicit unresolved upside total tail, not silently excluded.
- **Baseline_P, p_card, score distribution widths, overtime frequency, covariance, possession count:** `NOT_COMPUTED / NOT_VERIFIED` for this exact league/regime. No simulation performed.

## A5. Contract-specific failure branches and sensitivity

1. **Creighton −3.5:** wins if Creighton wins by 4+ after any OT. Drivers: two early clean home wins, Enis production, more steals/defensive pressure. Failure: K-State's home perimeter conversion, Creighton first away appearance and new rotation.
2. **Kansas State +3.5:** wins on K-State win or defeat by 1–3; loses on Creighton margin 4+. Drivers: home court and Rechsteiner shooting; failure: K-State's shot-selection volatility and an early deficit from turnovers.
3. **Over 159.5:** 160+ combined. Needs fast conversion, elevated second-half pace/fouls, high 3PT or overtime. This is a plausible but not established state.
4. **Under 159.5:** ≤159 points. Supported by 137, 138 and 134 totals in three pregame matches, Creighton half-court defending and a raw 145.5 diagnostic. Main failure: K-State's 17-of-41 3-point form against CSUN, late fouling, overtime, or Creighton's early defence regressing against better competition.
5. **Winner:** slight Creighton lean; do not confuse projected winner with a successful −3.5 point cover.
6. Any correlation between the preferred Under and preferred Creighton handicap is unmeasured; don't treat as independent legs.

## A4. Rates, reference and arithmetic (descriptive diagnostics only)

1. **Controlled 160–170 total**: both season defences ~79–80 PA, Hiroshima's last two 153 and 147, and prior two meetings 165/137 support Under 170.5.

4. **Hiroshima separates**: 13–1 record, big-man rebounding and extra rest might create a >2-point home victory, but the necessary player and rest-effect parameters are unestimated.

There is no larger H2H sample. Tasmania has won both home games at two Tasmanian venues, while Southside has lost both away games (limited n). Last outings: Tasmania 7 Nov (5 days rest), Southside 8 Nov (4 days rest); Flyers travel from Victoria to Launceston. Venue indoors: no external weather adjustment. Different player availability, rebounding and offensive volatility make previous H2H far from dispositive. Coach styles / precise current pace and possession ratings not independently established at the requested horizon.

## A6. Descriptive arithmetic (NOT a v24.16 primary probability)

- Tasmania offence = `(103+77+93+68)/4 = 85.25`; opposition points allowed = `(90+91+85+100)/4 = 91.5`.
- Southside offence = `(73+91+69+74+69)/5 = 75.2`; allowed = `(77+77+74+81+86)/5 = 79.0`.
- Cross-match simple means: Tasmania `(85.25+79.0)/2 = 82.125`; Southside `(75.2+91.5)/2 = 83.35`; total **165.475**, nominal Southside by **1.225** (unadjusted and **not** the projected final score).
- Across these two teams, 4 of 9 individual prior game totals exceeded 165.5 (Tas 4/4; Southside 1/5), but H2H is counted twice and these outcomes aren't independent; don't treat this as a hit-rate probability.
- Home vs away total contexts diverge: Tasmania home n2 total 185.5, Southside away n2 total 149.0. Regress both rather than extrapolate tiny-sample extremes.
- The user's 165.5 total is effectively on the naive 165.475 centre, so **neither Over nor Under has a reliable directional edge from these means**.
- The 8.5-point handicap is much less restrictive for Southside than its ML, and the only head-to-head favoured Southside, but Tasmania's home results and two returning/debuting bigs are material adverse scenarios for +8.5.

No independent paired WNBL model was fitted, no calibrated reference produced, no Kit `PRIMARY` receipt verified, and no Monte Carlo simulation executed. The league prior from repo development data would be a proxy, not independently qualified, and is not silently priced.

## A1. Event identity, timing and state

- Match: Polona Hercog (SLO) v Varvara Lepchenko (USA); WTA 125 DIRECTV Open Presentado por Compesar / Cundinamarca Open, Round 1, women's singles, Court 2, Sopó, Colombia; outdoor clay, standard best-of-three singles expected. Reference WTA competition page: https://www.wtatennis.com/tournaments/1181/sopo-125/2026
- Primary published match schedule: Monday **9 Nov 2026, 15:00 UTC**, equivalent to **Monday 9 Nov 10:00 local Colombia (UTC−5)** / **Tuesday 10 Nov 02:00 Australia/Melbourne (AEDT)**. ## A2. Participants and availability

- Hercog: age 35, right-handed; recent appearances at Queretaro (Oct 27 and 29), Cali (Nov 1 and 2). Her most recent completed match was a **0–6, 1–6 loss** to Leyre Romero Gormaz on Nov 2. Previous recorded retirements include Kitzbuhel (16 July) and Rabat (18 May), not evidence of a current injury.
- Lepchenko: age 40, left-handed; on Oct 18 and 19 she won Austin qualifying against Cristina Ramos Sierra and Christasha McNeil; on Oct 20 she **withdrew before** the W50 Austin main-draw match against Martina Capurro Taborda. Pooled descriptive 9/20 over, 11/20 under; **these are not forecast probabilities**. ## A4. Joint match process, forecast reference and calculations

- Full repository reference tier: `NOT_COMPUTED`; reference stage `NOT_RUN`; declared league width floor `NOT_VERIFIED`; expected games by player / expected total / expected sets / P(deciding set): `NOT_COMPUTED`; numerical fit/simulation: `NOT_RUN`.

No unsupported branch weights have been assigned.
- Illustrative *scenarios, not predictions*: Hercog **6–4, 6–4** gives aggregate +4 games and 20 total: Hercog ML, Hercog −1.5, Under 21.5. Hercog **6–4, 3–6, 6–4** gives aggregate +1 and 29 total: Hercog ML but **Lepchenko +1.5 games** and **Over**. Lepchenko **6–3, 6–4** yields Lepchenko ML, Lepchenko +1.5 and Under. These demonstrate that ML and game handicap are not identical, and a third set changes the total strongly.
- The raw full-season clay record favours Hercog, while the 2 Nov surface Elo benchmark is almost flat. Both sides therefore retain plausible two-set and deciding-set win paths; low confidence / broad uncertainty is compulsory.

## A5. Exact contracts and directional comparison

- Winner: Hercog ML versus Lepchenko ML; neither has a certified p. Directional preference Hercog **very slight only**. No draw in a completed best-of-three singles match.
- **Games handicap, not set handicap:** Hercog −1.5 games succeeds if (total games Hercog − total games Lepchenko) > 1.5 at the applicable settlement endpoint; Lepchenko +1.5 succeeds when that difference is < 1.5. Half-game line has no push after an ordinary completed match. The −1.5 game cover must never be assumed just because Hercog wins a three-set match.
- Combined games O/U **21.5**: Over when sum of ordinary games >= 22; Under when <= 21; tiebreak **points** are excluded (a 7–6 set contains 13 games). No push on a half-game total after normal match completion.
- Low-confidence qualitative ranking: **Hercog ML; Hercog −1.5 games; Under 21.5 games**. The exact ordering of the last two is unproven; **NO_STRONG_PICK / ABSTAIN** governs. No unverified claim of >65% selection confidence or expected betting value.

## A6. Court, weather, format and settlement

- Outdoor clay, 32-player singles main draw; 125-level tournament; first round. A venue-local forecast page suggested temperatures near 19°C and modest wind around local 10:00, but neither court-level conditions nor rain interruption was independently corroborated. Weather adjustment: **NOT_ESTIMATED**. Source: https://www.meteocity.com/colombie/sopo-v3667807 .
- Expected standard advantage-scoring best of three sets with regular tiebreak at 6–6; exact deciding-set rules and operator-specific definitions were **NOT_VERIFIED**. Do not substitute a 10-point match tiebreak for a normal third set without proof.
- **Operator retirement/walkover terms UNKNOWN_DEFINITION:** Moneyline, game handicap and total may void or settle differently if either player withdraws/retires. A sporting advancement result does not establish the wager outcome. No wager is recommended on an unstated operator convention.

## A7. Bust paths / sensitivity

1. Hercog's recent **0–6, 1–6** loss signals possible deterioration or matchup variance, which could overwhelm her historical clay edge.
2. Lepchenko's **20 October withdrawal** is a genuine participation/fitness risk, but cause and persistence are unknown. Do not call her injured.
3. Opponent strength and qualifying-level differences can reverse raw W–L comparisons. The independent clay Elo gap is only 4.1 points.
4. A competitive tiebreak or any ordinary deciding-set match may send the total Over 21.5 despite a straight-sets/Under lean.
5. A three-set Hercog win with just a +1 game margin wins the ML but loses −1.5 games.
6. Delay, rain, late withdrawal or mid-match retirement could alter match exposure and all contract settlements.

## A5 — Joint-score numerical reference attempt / scenario

**Model reference stage:** `NOT_RUN` — repo source exists on GitHub in read-only mode, but the operator's local working tree, current data snapshots, and required `python -m ml reference tier`, `reference run`, `card lint`, `pregame run` receipts are not present. No Monte Carlo, simulation, fitting, calibrated probability or issued p_card may be claimed.

**Descriptive, non-primary arithmetic only:**
- Perth current offense 262/3 = 87.33; defense 264/3 = 88.00.
- Bendigo current offense 313/4 = 78.25; defense 359/4 = 89.75.
- Naive equal-weight offense/opponent-defense crossmatch: Perth = (87.33 + 89.75)/2 = **88.54**; Bendigo = (78.25 + 88.00)/2 = **83.13**; total = **171.67**; margin = **Perth +5.42**. This diagnostic lacks league normalization, opponent adjustment, home strength, current availability-mixture coefficients, pace, possessions, covariance, overtime, and a reference width. It must NOT be promoted to a forecast or read as the p_card anchor.
- Alternative qualitative routes: (i) depleted Bendigo halfcourt scoring and slow pace => Under and a potentially large Perth margin; (ii) Perth transition success versus short Bendigo bench => higher Perth points, Perth cover/Over; (iii) Bendigo's Jade Melbourne offense sustaining competitive periods => +10.5 Bendigo and potentially Over; (iv) Perth early fouls/bench rotations => narrower margin regardless of total.
- No declared numeric scenario weights, SDs, expected scoring intervals, reference p, baseline p, or W/L probability masses could be evidenced. They remain `NOT_COMPUTED` / `NOT_ESTIMATED`, not 0.5. The supplied lines are contract descriptions, not odds or probabilities.

## A6 — Winner, spread, total and interdependence

- Winner direction: Perth (home, roster availability, rest, Bendigo 0–4), uncalibrated and not a supported probability.
- Perth −10.5: Requires winning by **11 or more**, including OT. Perth's 2026–27 wins were by 3 and 8, but over different opponents and split venues. Bendigo lost its four games by 2, 23, 7 and 14, all outside Perth. The large handicap does not follow merely from a likely Perth win.
- Bendigo +10.5: Covers on a Bendigo victory or a Perth win by 1–10. If Bendigo's shortened rotation sustains offense and defensive rebounding, this remains plausible. A decisive Perth run and thin Bendigo bench can break the cushion.
- Over 167.5: Current Perth games 169, 172, 185 (3/3 Over); Bendigo 186, 159, 147, 180 (2/4 Over). The naive unadjusted matchup arithmetic is 171.7. Both the historical head-to-head 159 average and Bendigo's low-scoring losses challenge that signal.
- Under 167.5: Supported by all four recent prior-season H2Hs and two Bendigo current games, but the large post-season changes to both teams preclude treating those results as a current pace model.
- Dependence: Perth −10.5 and the Under can both win through Bendigo offensive suppression; Perth −10.5 and Over can both win through Perth efficiency; Bendigo +10.5 and Over can coexist in a competitive high-scoring game. Multiple directions are NOT independent legs and are NOT recommended as a parlay.

**Authority and scope.** Sports-Research `main` at commit `f79559b45183ca63a2ce5fc2611321975bae744f`, read through the connected GitHub integration exclusively using read actions. Current published controls: MDS-2026.11.10-v24.12; CR-2026.11.10-R2; P-2026.11.10-v24.10; SCV-2026.10.22-v12; SEL-2026.10.22-v4. Calculations described as descriptive heuristics are not the repository reference engine. Formal source-audit, league-reference, linter and model were **NOT_RUN**.

## A1. Event identity / timing

- **Fixture:** Ulsan Hyundai Mobis Phoebus (home) vs Goyang Sono Skygunners (away), Korean Basketball League, 2026–27 regular season. Match identifier reported by StatGG: `S49G01N63`.
- **Scheduled:** Tuesday, 10 November 2026, 19:00 KST (10:00 UTC; 21:00 AEDT). Venue: Dongcheon Gymnasium, Ulsan, South Korea. Official KBL club site and the contemporary Jumpball 10 November preview support the fixture.
- **Actual first play:** NOT_VERIFIED. A local clock read 21:00:48 AEDT after the scheduled start during research. Consequently, **not claimed as a pre-start-issued forecast**. There was no in-game scoring deliberately incorporated. Any later review must use the independently verified actual first-play timestamp; do not backdate issue or inputs.
- **State on the pre-match sources:** listed as scheduled; this is not a claim that the game remained unstarted at final document time. No current-game score or result is used.

## A2. Supplied contract inventory and descriptive dispositions

All supplied full-game contracts include overtime per the owner's BK-OT-1 convention. KBL regulation: four 10-minute quarters, followed by 5-minute overtime periods when necessary. No separate operator-specific void/settlement agreement was supplied.

| Over 175.5 points | Both teams combine for 176+ | Near-even competing evidence | NOT_ESTIMATED |
| Under 175.5 points | Both teams combine for 175 or fewer | **VERY SLIGHT LEAN**, not independent of injury/pace scenario | NOT_ESTIMATED |

| Mobis match winner (informational) | Mobis wins including OT | Credible home upset | NOT_ESTIMATED |

**Preferred supplied contract if forced to choose:** Mobis +3.0 (low conviction). **Formal framework decision:** `NO_STRONG_PICK / ABSTAIN`; no supported reference or normalized distribution / probabilities produced. A Goyang 88–86 Mobis outcome (174 combined) is only a structural illustration: Goyang ML, Mobis +3.0 and Under 175.5 can all win together; it is NOT a predicted score.

## A3. Roster, availability, minutes, confirmed players

- **Mobis:** Gaige Prim and Darius Bazley were reported out with injuries assessed around six weeks each. Replacement import Omari Spellman played two games, averaging 28.0 points, 8.0 rebounds and approximately 39 minutes, including 33 points against Anyang on 8 November. Lee Woo-suk's season average is 15.1 points. The 10 November specialist preview says the second import replacement had not yet been secured. Exact starting five and active bench for this event: **NOT_VERIFIED**.
- **Sono:** Scotty James was confirmed sidelined with a lumbar transverse process fracture (about six weeks). Johnny O'Bryant resumed team training before 7 November and played that game; the specialist 10 November preview expects him to be Sono's only active foreign player. This supersedes an older or stale aggregator marking him 'questionable'. Lee Jung-hyun scored double digits in nine consecutive games, averaging about 21 over that stretch. Choi Seung-wook missed 7 November because of a calf injury; exact 10 November status: **NOT_VERIFIED**. Exact starting five and active bench: **NOT_VERIFIED**.
- **Availability effect:** both teams have lost import depth; Mobis relies heavily on Spellman, while Sono's 3-point attack and O'Bryant/Lee Jung-hyun remain available as of specialist reporting. This fact alone has no empirically validated KBL point adjustment in this session; no numerical injury effect is claimed.

## A5. Descriptive calculation and contract dependence

- **Cross-match season scoring:** Mobis estimated PF = (Mobis PF 83.5 + Sono PA 90.8)/2 = 87.15. Sono estimated PF = (Sono PF 89.6 + Mobis PA 88.7)/2 = 89.15. **Total 176.30**, Sono margin +2.00. This double-counts shared league context and does not adjust today's rosters.
- **Pace / efficiency alternative:** Assumed blended pace (75.1+72.9)/2 = 74 possessions. Mobis points ~74×(108.4+121.1)/(2×100) = 84.915. Sono ~74×(118.8+115.2)/(2×100) = 86.58. **Total ~171.50**, Sono margin ~+1.67. Only a rough arithmetic check, not a calibrated possession model; published ratings can use different pace denominators.
- **L10 totals:** Mobis 83.0+87.9=170.9, Sono 91.8+92.4=184.2. Equal-weight descriptive midpoint **177.55**. The L5 combined means are **168.2** and **195.0**, so the midpoint **181.6** overweights the same short-term window and a previous roster regime.
- **Count comparison to line:** Mobis L5: 2 Over / 3 Under 175.5. Sono L5: 4 Over / 1 Under 175.5. H2H 177, barely Over. These are dependent descriptive counts, not a model-based probability.
- **Representative joint states (unweighted, not forecasts):** Sono 88–86 Mobis (174 total, Mobis +3 and Under), Sono 94–85 Mobis (179, Sono −3 and Over), Mobis 89–85 Sono (174, Mobis +3 and Under), Sono 91–88 Mobis (179; spread PUSH, total Over). No weights because an empirical and independently qualified point/margin distribution is unavailable.
- **Overtime:** user-provided lines include OT; explicit tie mass, OT possessions and correlations not estimated, which is a material unresolved numerical issue.
- **No numeric P/W/P/L:** a proper reference computation, verified minute-level roster weights, common variance parameters and actual outcome state were not obtained. Any numeric winner/spread/total probabilities would be invented.

## A6. Coaching, tactics, playing conditions

- Mobis coach Yang Dong-geun; Sono coach Son Chang-hwan, as credited in 10 November BasketKorea preview. Mobis' limited import depth and 40-minute reliance on Spellman creates high minutes/fatigue sensitivity. Sono leads the KBL in 3-point volume according to Jumpball, averaging 14.6 made threes across its first 13 games, 36.5%; Mobis has allowed a league-low 8.6 made threes/game but gave up 16 to Sono on 16 October. This is a material matchup/regime conflict and widens the total/side outlook.
- Four 10-minute quarters; full-game overtime included under BK-OT-1. Late fouls, import substitutions, poor defensive rebounding, free throws and OT can lift the total. A slower half-court/one-import alignment can lower the total.
- Weather is irrelevant to an indoor match; travel/rest information above lacks a measured adjustment. No referee assignment verified, and no precise last-minute starter list verified.

## A4. Goalies, roster and personnel status

**Starting goaltenders confirmed**, as of the 10 November reported lineup updates: Mackenzie Blackwood COL (9–3–1; 2.60 GAA, .901 SV%), Sergei Bobrovsky TOR (3–7–2; 3.34 GAA, .886 SV%). Confirmation timestamps on DailyFaceoff were 2026-11-10 18:20:53Z and 15:33:58Z respectively, published prior to this request; confirmed on https://www.dailyfaceoff.com/starting-goalies/2026-11-10 and supported by https://coloradohockeynow.com/avalanche-maple-leafs-game-preview-lines-how-to-watch/ . Blackwood made 27 saves in the 4–0 shutout versus Detroit 7 Nov.

No confirmed skater dressed-state receipt. **Colorado injuries:** Josh Manson lower body/ankle, Zachary L'Heureux upper body. Cale Makar expected to play despite a precautionary early practice departure.

**Toronto projected lines:** Cowan–Matthews–Roslovic; Tverberg–Tavares–Nylander; Joshua–Paul–McKenna; Duhaime–Blueger–MacEwen. Defence Ekman-Larsson–Tanev; McCabe–Raddysh; Mermis–Stecher. Injuries: Max Domi, Kirill Marchenko, Marshall Rifai, Nick Blankenburg, Morgan Rielly and Colton Sissons. **Jack Roslovic conflict:** NHL.com tags him injured but also projects him on the starting top line; contemporaneous reporter says returning. Treat playing status as `PROJECTED_RETURN / NOT_FINAL_CONFIRMED`, not absent and not confirmed dressed. Chris Tanev returning from load management. ## A5. H2H, venue, schedule and market dependence

- October 10 first meeting of 2026–27: **Toronto 6–3 Colorado** in Colorado; total 9 goals, Colorado ML lost, Toronto +1.5 covered, Over 6.5 won. This is one game under another goalie and roster regime, not a deterministic rule.
- Last known Colorado result DET shutout Nov 7; Toronto lost NJD 0–4 Nov 8 and entered on three straight defeats. Colorado starts a three-game Canadian road trip. Neither team on a back-to-back; Toronto has a little less rest but remains at home. Colorado home/away split before the game: road 3–5–0 and home 7–1–2 per available season stats, a caution against road favourite certainty.
- Colorado ML and Toronto +1.5 can **both win** on a one-goal Colorado victory. A Toronto outright win defeats Colorado ML but covers Toronto +1.5; a Colorado multi-goal win defeats the cushion. One-goal losses, 3v3 overtime, shootout, empty-net scores and trailing-goalie pull need joint-model treatment.
- Total Over 6.5 can be reached by 4–3, 5–2, 5–3 or 6–2; Under by 3–2, 4–2, 3–3 before OT resolution, etc. A high-event Toronto comeback might simultaneously favour Toronto cushion and Over; Colorado runaway can favour COL ML and Over or even Under if Toronto is suppressed. Empty net can add a late goal or wider margin. No independent-proposition assumption.

## A6. Descriptive scoring budgets (not forecast probabilities)

- Season cross-match arithmetic: COL point-rate midpoint `(COL GF~4.0 + TOR GA~3.3) / 2 ≈ 3.65`; TOR point-rate midpoint `(TOR GF~3.4 + COL GA~3.1) / 2 ≈ 3.25`; combined ≈**6.90**. This is a raw heuristic, **not** an NHL reference model and not a fitted or opponent-adjusted projection.
- L10 cross-match arithmetic: `(COL 3.6 + TOR conceded 3.2)/2 = 3.40`; `(TOR 3.5 + COL conceded 3.2)/2 = 3.35`; combined **6.75**.
- L5 cross-match arithmetic: `(COL 3.6 + TOR conceded 3.6)/2 = 3.60`; `(TOR 2.4 + COL conceded 2.8)/2 = 2.60`; combined **6.20**. Opposing regime direction warns against overstating the Over at 6.5.
- Reported goalies and unconfirmed forward rotations materially change tails. No true conditional hockey goal grid, correlation/covariance, Poisson rate calibrated to correct league reference, OT/SO branch, 6-on-5 empty-net branch, Monte Carlo, Poisson tail or 1-goal-margin mass was run; none is claimed.

No demonstrated ≥0.65 `p_card`, no confidence interval, no independent qualification. All supplied contracts assessed, **no official ranked pick**. ## A5 — Offence, defence, xG, special teams, baseline arithmetic and total logic

Season actual rates (team A at Abbotsford, team B Colorado):
- ABB goals scored=42/12=**3.5000**; conceded=32/12=**2.6667**. COL scored=40/14=**2.8571**; conceded=35/14=**2.5000**.
- **Plain opponent cross-matched actual-goal diagnostic:** projected ABB = (3.5000 + 2.5000)/2=**3.0000**; COL=(2.8571+2.6667)/2=**2.7619**; combined=**5.7619**, unadjusted. This is not an approved competition reference, and ignores opponent mix, goalies and outliers.
- **Expected-goals-strength diagnostic:** ABB xGF/60=2.85, COL xGA/60=2.90 -> mean ABB 2.875; COL xGF/60=2.65, ABB xGA/60=2.60 -> mean COL 2.625; combined **5.500**. This is an unadjusted average of reported xG inputs, **not** a score-grid, qualified model, or a forecast probability. Source: https://www.ahldashboard.com/teams/1168?tab=stats ; https://www.ahldashboard.com/teams/1176?tab=stats .
- Expected-goals-share: ABB **52.27%**, COL **47.74%**. Scoring-chance share ABB **53.14%**, COL **45.45%** (different share definitions/sources, so not constrained to sum to exactly 100%).
- Special teams: ABB PP **7/45=15.56%**, PK **33/39=84.62%**; COL PP **9/64=14.06%**, PK **53/61=86.89%**. Both PP rates modest; COL PK more effective, limiting ABB special-team upside. Special-team opportunity volume for this exact game NOT_VERIFIED; do not apply a fictional fixed goal shift.
- League-wide current AHL summary gives **5.83 goals/game in regulation**; the specific league/period cohort, full-game shootout-awarded goals and data lineage must be reconciled before a model prior. https://pitchlabstats.com/hockey-league/ahl .
- The 5.5 total sits between several close diagnostics (5.50 xG, 5.76 cross-match, 5.83 league). The near-horizon combined totals (COL L5 4.8, ABB L5 5.0) lean below the line but are a small, overlapping sample. ***Neither a robust Over nor a robust Under***. OT/SO and empty-net tails alter total 5.5 behaviour.
- **Numerical reference stage:** `python -m ml reference tier --competition AHL` and `reference run` NOT_EXECUTED; league-only baseline parameters NOT_FROZEN; no `ANALYST_PRIMARY_V1` p_card, code score grid, adjustment ledger, simulation seed/N, Monte Carlo error, game-level posterior or adjusted expected margin calculated. ## A6 — H2H, score-state dependence and rules

Two prior **2026–27 head-to-head** matches (Colorado home): **9 October COL 4–3 ABB (shootout)**, **10 October ABB 4–3 COL (regulation)**. Both ended with seven in listed final score. Independent corroboration: https://www.coloradoeagles.com/news/detail/eagles-roar-back-to-defeat-abbotsford-4-3-in-shootout and https://www.coloradoeagles.com/news/detail/eagles-come-up-short-in-4-3-loss-to-abbotsford , plus schedules above.

Winner has mutually exclusive home and away control / regulation-draw-to-OT branches; either team can win quickly or via OT. Total plausible paths include 3–2 ABB (ABB ML, Under 5.5), 4–2 ABB (ABB ML, Over 5.5), 3–2 COL (COL ML, Under 5.5), and 4–3 either team (Over 5.5). The moneyline and total are **not statistically independent**: trailing-team goalie pulls, empty-net goals, score state and OT/SO create correlated tails. Goals from shootout settlement depend on actual operator convention.

## A4. Distribution, reference and numerical diagnostics

**Reference stage:** `NOT_EXECUTED`; no local authorised working tree with executable ML reference receipt, no `python -m ml reference tier`, no competition baseline receipt, no official card; hence no published `ANALYST_PRIMARY_V1 p_card`, no Poisson/normal/Monte Carlo run, no uncertainty interval masquerading as statistical confidence. The repository `research/model_registry/ML_PROMOTIONS.csv` has only its header; no competition-specific fitted model is promoted.

**Raw scoring versus opposing allowance calculation (transparent, not a calibrated forecast):**
- Creighton descriptive scoring centre: `(79.5 scored + 73.0 Kansas State conceded)/2 = 76.25`.
- Kansas State descriptive scoring centre: `(80.5 scored + 58.0 Creighton conceded)/2 = 69.25`.
- Descriptive sum: **145.50**, margin Creighton **+7.00**. The distance from 159.5 is **14.0 points**.
- **Major limitation:** Creighton defence is based on Kansas City and Le Moyne only; Kansas State scored 95 against CSUN with a high-variance 17/41 3PT performance, while Creighton is playing on the road for the first time. Neither an established opponent-adjusted efficiency baseline nor home uplift has been estimated. This centre is a *diagnostic*, not a rankable forecast.
- An Under and Creighton cover can coexist (e.g. 79–73, total 152, margin 6); a Kansas State hot shooting/fast-paced branch can produce Over and Kansas State +3.5. They are dependent alternatives. Overtime is an explicit unresolved upside total tail, not silently excluded.
- **Baseline_P, p_card, score distribution widths, overtime frequency, covariance, possession count:** `NOT_COMPUTED / NOT_VERIFIED` for this exact league/regime. No simulation performed.

## A5. Contract-specific failure branches and sensitivity

1. **Creighton −3.5:** wins if Creighton wins by 4+ after any OT. Drivers: two early clean home wins, Enis production, more steals/defensive pressure. Failure: K-State's home perimeter conversion, Creighton first away appearance and new rotation.
2. **Kansas State +3.5:** wins on K-State win or defeat by 1–3; loses on Creighton margin 4+. Drivers: home court and Rechsteiner shooting; failure: K-State's shot-selection volatility and an early deficit from turnovers.
3. **Over 159.5:** 160+ combined. Needs fast conversion, elevated second-half pace/fouls, high 3PT or overtime. This is a plausible but not established state.
4. **Under 159.5:** ≤159 points. Supported by 137, 138 and 134 totals in three pregame matches, Creighton half-court defending and a raw 145.5 diagnostic. Main failure: K-State's 17-of-41 3-point form against CSUN, late fouling, overtime, or Creighton's early defence regressing against better competition.
5. **Winner:** slight Creighton lean; do not confuse projected winner with a successful −3.5 point cover.
6. Any correlation between the preferred Under and preferred Creighton handicap is unmeasured; don't treat as independent legs.

Retrieval around 11 November 2026 12:51–12:55 AEDT; exact per-item retrieval timestamps not persisted. Parent repos/issuer remain read-only.

## A9. Research completion matrix, delivery and custody

| Coverage field | Status / exact missingness |
|---|---|
| A1 fixture/time/venue/contracts | DONE, pregame verified |
| A2 all supplied contract directions | DONE, qualitative; paired probabilities not available |

**Classification:** `PARTIAL_RESEARCH / UNCALIBRATED_QUALITATIVE / NOT_ISSUED`, not performance eligible. The proposed draft key is for local organization **only**. For any later authorized import, confirm live state, duplicate identity, the published mini's writer lease and no outcome exposure, then run competition reference, source audits and all required freeze/linter and learning-log processes before any eligible issuance. This draft cannot be converted retrospectively into a frozen pre-tip forecast.

# Six previous game forecasts — v24.15 retrospective format conversion

**Created (UTC):** `2026-11-11T08:44:21+00:00` · **Current GitHub main (read-only):** `4d8bd1771d20974bcbfc7c694a009cd44e95a693` · **Method:** `MDS-2026.11.11-v24.15 / CR-2026.11.11-R3 / P-2026.11.11-v24.13 / EXT-v3 / REQ-v5 / SR-KIT-v24.15`.

**Hard boundary:** This package is a retrospective **format conversion** of six previous unissued qualitative research predictions. It is not six prospective / certified / performance-eligible v24.15 forecasts. It does **not** execute EXT-v3 admission, historical reference/kit calculation, official appends, scoring or settlement. No game results were looked up.

## Register (original directional picks preserved)

| Draft | Match | Original preferred direction | Other qualitative lean | Contract rows | Eligible as v24.15 issued forecast? |
|---|---|---|---|---:|---|
| DRAFT-001 | Polona Hercog vs Varvara Lepchenko | **Hercog match winner (ML)** | Under 21.5 games — marginal original secondary lean | 6 | **No** — retro research only |
| DRAFT-002 | Perth Lynx (W) vs Bendigo Spirit (W) | **Perth Lynx -10.5 points** | Over 167.5 points — slight original secondary lean | 4 | **No** — retro research only |
| DRAFT-003 | Ulsan Hyundai Mobis Phoebus vs Goyang Sono Skygunners | **Mobis Phoebus +3.0 points** | Under 175.5 points — very slight original secondary lean | 4 | **No** — retro research only |
| DRAFT-004 | Colorado Avalanche @ Toronto Maple Leafs | **Colorado Avalanche ML** | Over 6.5 goals — slight original secondary lean | 4 | **No** — retro research only |
| DRAFT-005 | Colorado Eagles @ Abbotsford Canucks | **Abbotsford Canucks ML** | Under 5.5 goals — very slight original secondary lean | 4 | **No** — retro research only |
| DRAFT-006 | Creighton Bluejays @ Kansas State Wildcats | **Under 159.5 points** | Creighton -3.5 points — marginal original secondary lean | 4 | **No** — retro research only |

**Total:** 6 matches / **26 exact directions**; each direction has `UNPRICED / UNAVAILABLE` with a reason; all original **NO_STRONG_PICK / ABSTAIN** verdicts retained.

## What the conversion actually does

- Reconciles home/away identity, scheduled time, venue, full-match period, overtime/shootout/retirement assumptions, push and void definitions with the original records.
- Copies each entire original A1–A9 research document verbatim and records its SHA-256; retains the original timestamp and framework label.
- Adds v24.15 A1–A9 retrofit metadata, contract-by-contract direction, coverage/disposition table, explicit timing/lineup/model/source gates, and an audit that the original v24.15 manifests, genuine lanes, and numerical receipts were absent.
- Creates no EV2 key, P-ID, allocated LOCAL key, official mini append, settlement, grades, market odds, model probability, new result lookup or forecast issuance.
